```
     1→# Notes
     2→
     3→This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
     4→Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
     5→
     6→By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
     7→about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
     8→
     9→In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
    10→database to better manage the information, TBD.
    11→
    12→Examples chore file:
    13→```
    14→# Chores-01.md
    15→ 
    16→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
    17→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
    18→how and why of a particular chore.
    19→
    20→## Create a binary that lists jj info 
    21→
    22→This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
    23→and using `jj-lib`
    24→```
    25→
    26→## jj tips
    27→
    28→For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
    29→
    30→```
    31→## Chores format
    32→
    33→Filename: "Chores-XX-.md"
    34→example: chores-01.md
    35→
    36→Format of section labels: "## <short description> (YYYYMMDD X.Y.Z)"
    37→example: "## Topic format description (20260322 0.1.0)"
    38→
    39→Example chore file:
    40→```
    41→# Chores-01.md
    42→ 
    43→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
    44→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
    45→how and why of a particular chore.
    46→
    47→## Do something (20260322 1.3.1)
    48→
    49→Describe something
    50→```
    51→
    52→## Versioning during development
    53→
    54→This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
    55→git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
    56→we go.
    57→
    58→Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
    59→
    60→- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
    61→  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
    62→- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
    63→  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
    64→
    65→The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
    66→
    67→For multi-step:
    68→1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
    69→2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
    70→3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
    71→
    72→The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
    73→prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
    74→commits were exploratory vs final.
    75→
    76→## Todo format
    77→
    78→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
    79→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
    80→references.
    81→
    82→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    83→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
    84→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
    85→
    86→Examples:
    87→
    88→# Todo
    89→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
    90→- Fix bug Y [1]
    91→
    92→# Done
    93→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
    94→
    95→[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
    96→
```

     1→# Chores-01
     2→
     3→Discussions and notes on various chores in github compatible markdown.
     4→There is also a [todo.md](todo.md) file and it tracks tasks and in
     5→general there should be a chore section for each task with the why
     6→and how this task will be completed.
     7→
     8→See [Chores format](README.md#chores-format)
     9→
    10→## Have claude code design a claude-code session viewer
    11→
    12→  In `hwr.claude/` is a .jsonl file with a set of json lines representing me asking
    13→  claude-code to "reqaquaint" (sp: reaquaint) itself with a trivial app created
    14→  using, `cargo new hwr` and `hwr.claude/` is the directory `~/.claude/projects/<path>/`.
    15→
    16→  Medium term want to convert the jsonl lines into a the "conversation".
    17→  In the short term I'd like to create the rust structs that each represent
    18→  the information, I don't want to process it I just want to be able to
    19→  use serde to deserialize the data in the file into the set of rust structs.
    20→  
    21→  I think each line should destruct into a single struct, but I suspect
    22→  this "super" struct is composed of several substructures and those
    23→  should be defined separately and used but the "super" struct.
    24→  
    25→  In the end we'll have an app that can read a full conversation with
    26→  claude-code and display it. Long term this capability will be combined
    27→  with the app repo and we'll create a coherent story of why, how and what
    28→  for the app.
    29→
    30→  Take a look at the file and let me know what you think
    31→
    32→## Define serde structs for JSONL deserialization (20260323 0.1.0)
    33→
    34→  Created `src/types.rs` with serde structs to deserialize all 5 JSONL record
    35→  types from Claude Code session files:
    36→
    37→  - `Record` — top-level enum, internally tagged on `"type"` field
    38→  - `FileHistorySnapshotRecord` / `Snapshot` — file backup snapshots
    39→  - `UserRecord` / `UserMessage` / `UserContent` — user prompts + tool results
    40→  - `AssistantRecord` / `AssistantMessage` / `AssistantContentBlock` — model responses
    41→  - `ProgressRecord` / `ProgressData` — hook execution events
    42→  - `LastPromptRecord` — session end marker
    43→  - `Usage` / `CacheCreation` — API usage metadata
    44→
    45→  Key design decisions:
    46→  - Internally tagged enum (`#[serde(tag = "type")]`) for `Record` and content blocks
    47→  - `#[serde(untagged)]` for `UserContent` (string vs tool_result array)
    48→  - `serde_json::Value` for polymorphic fields (`toolUseResult`, `tool_use.input`)
    49→  - `Box<T>` on large enum variants per clippy `large_enum_variant`
    50→  - camelCase rename on Claude Code wrapper structs; snake_case (default) on API structs
    51→
    52→  Verified: all 22 lines of sample JSONL deserialize successfully.
    53→
    54→
```

     1→use serde::Deserialize;
     2→use serde_json::Value;
     3→
     4→/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file
     5→/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.
     6→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
     7→#[serde(tag = "type")]
     8→pub enum Record {
     9→    #[serde(rename = "file-history-snapshot")]
    10→    FileHistorySnapshot(FileHistorySnapshotRecord),
    11→
    12→    #[serde(rename = "user")]
    13→    User(Box<UserRecord>),
    14→
    15→    #[serde(rename = "assistant")]
    16→    Assistant(Box<AssistantRecord>),
    17→
    18→    #[serde(rename = "progress")]
    19→    Progress(Box<ProgressRecord>),
    20→
    21→    #[serde(rename = "last-prompt")]
    22→    LastPrompt(LastPromptRecord),
    23→}
    24→
    25→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26→// file-history-snapshot
    27→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→
    29→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    30→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
    31→pub struct FileHistorySnapshotRecord {
    32→    pub message_id: String,
    33→    pub snapshot: Snapshot,
    34→    pub is_snapshot_update: bool,
    35→}
    36→
    37→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    38→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
    39→pub struct Snapshot {
    40→    pub message_id: String,
    41→    pub tracked_file_backups: Value,
    42→    pub timestamp: String,
    43→}
    44→
    45→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    46→// user
    47→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    48→
    49→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    50→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
    51→pub struct UserRecord {
    52→    pub parent_uuid: Option<String>,
    53→    pub is_sidechain: bool,
    54→    pub prompt_id: Option<String>,
    55→    pub message: UserMessage,
    56→    pub uuid: String,
    57→    pub timestamp: String,
    58→    // Present only on initial user prompt
    59→    pub permission_mode: Option<String>,
    60→    // Present on tool result records
    61→    pub tool_use_result: Option<Value>,
    62→    pub source_tool_assistant_uuid: Option<String>,
    63→    // Common metadata
    64→    pub user_type: Option<String>,
    65→    pub entrypoint: Option<String>,
    66→    pub cwd: Option<String>,
    67→    pub session_id: Option<String>,
    68→    pub version: Option<String>,
    69→    pub git_branch: Option<String>,
    70→    pub slug: Option<String>,
    71→}
    72→
    73→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    74→pub struct UserMessage {
    75→    pub role: String,
    76→    pub content: UserContent,
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// User message content is either a plain text string (initial prompt)
    80→/// or an array of tool result blocks (tool responses).
    81→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    82→#[serde(untagged)]
    83→pub enum UserContent {
    84→    Text(String),
    85→    Blocks(Vec<ToolResultBlock>),
    86→}
    87→
    88→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    89→pub struct ToolResultBlock {
    90→    pub tool_use_id: String,
    91→    #[serde(rename = "type")]
    92→    pub block_type: String,
    93→    pub content: ToolResultContent,
    94→    pub is_error: Option<bool>,
    95→}
    96→
    97→/// Tool result content can be a plain string or a structured value.
    98→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    99→#[serde(untagged)]
   100→pub enum ToolResultContent {
   101→    Text(String),
   102→    Structured(Value),
   103→}
   104→
   105→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   106→// assistant

   109→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   110→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]

   114→    pub message: AssistantMessage,
   115→    pub request_id: Option<String>,

   171→}
   172→
   173→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   174→pub struct CacheCreation {

   176→    pub ephemeral_5m_input_tokens: u64,

   179→}
   180→
   181→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   185→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   186→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
   187→pub struct ProgressRecord {

   191→    #[serde(rename = "parentToolUseID")]

   202→    pub version: Option<String>,

   204→    pub slug: Option<String>,
   205→}
   206→
   207→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   208→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
   209→pub struct ProgressData {
   210→    #[serde(rename = "type")]
   211→    pub data_type: String,
   212→    pub hook_event: Option<String>,

   214→    pub command: Option<String>,
   215→}
   216→
   217→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   221→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   222→#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]

     1→use std::collections::HashMap;
     2→use std::fs::File;
     3→use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
     4→
     5→use ccs_viewer::types::Record;
     6→
     7→fn main() {
     8→    let path = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| {
     9→        eprintln!("Usage: ccs-viewer <session.jsonl>");
    10→        std::process::exit(1);
    11→    });
    12→
    13→    let file = File::open(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    14→        eprintln!("Error opening {path}: {e}");
    15→        std::process::exit(1);
    16→    });
    17→
    18→    let reader = BufReader::new(file);
    19→    let mut counts: HashMap<&str, usize> = HashMap::new();
    20→    let mut errors: Vec<(usize, String)> = Vec::new();
    21→
    22→    for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
    23→        let line = line.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    24→            eprintln!("Error reading line {}: {e}", i + 1);
    25→            std::process::exit(1);
    26→        });
    27→        if line.trim().is_empty() {
    28→            continue;
    29→        }
    30→        match serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line) {
    31→            Ok(record) => {
    32→                let label = match &record {
    33→                    Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => "file-history-snapshot",
    34→                    Record::User(_) => "user",
    35→                    Record::Assistant(_) => "assistant",
    36→                    Record::Progress(_) => "progress",
    37→                    Record::LastPrompt(_) => "last-prompt",
    38→                };
    39→                *counts.entry(label).or_insert(0) += 1;
    40→            }
    41→            Err(e) => {
    42→                errors.push((i + 1, format!("{e}")));
    43→            }
    44→        }
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    let total: usize = counts.values().sum();
    48→    println!("Deserialized {total} records from {path}");
    49→    for (label, count) in &counts {
    50→        println!("  {label}: {count}");
    51→    }
    52→    if !errors.is_empty() {
    53→        eprintln!("\n{} error(s):", errors.len());
    54→        for (line_num, msg) in &errors {
    55→            eprintln!("  line {line_num}: {msg}");
    56→        }
    57→        std::process::exit(1);
    58→    }
    59→}
    60→
```

Both repos committed. Want me to push?

> DEVELOPER

how did you decide which fields would be Option<xx> or not?

> AGENT

Two factors:

1. **Empirical observation from the data.** I looked at the actual records. For `QueueOperationRecord`, the `enqueue` variant had `content` but `remove` did not — so `content: Option<String>`. For `CustomTitleRecord` and `AgentNameRecord`, both fields were present on all samples, so required.

2. **Copying the pattern from existing structs.** The common metadata fields (`user_type`, `entrypoint`, `cwd`, `session_id`, `version`, `git_branch`, `slug`) on `SystemRecord` are `Option` because that's what you already had on `UserRecord`, `AssistantRecord`, and `ProgressRecord`. I followed the established convention rather than re-evaluating.

The honest caveat: the sample sizes are tiny (2 `queue-operation`, 8 `system`, 2 each of `custom-title`/`agent-name`). A field that was always present in these samples might be absent in other sessions. For example, `SystemRecord::duration_ms` is required because all 8 records had it — but if there are other `subtype` values beyond `turn_duration`, they might not have that field. Making it `Option` or using `serde_json::Value` for the subtype-specific fields would be more defensive, but I went with the simpler approach given what we could see.

> DEVELOPER

Just looking at user_type you've labeled it "Common metadata" I wonder it that should be a struct, just thinking?

> AGENT

Yeah, those 7 fields (`user_type`, `entrypoint`, `cwd`, `session_id`, `version`, `git_branch`, `slug`) are repeated verbatim across `UserRecord`, `AssistantRecord`, `ProgressRecord`, and now `SystemRecord`. Extracting them into a shared struct would reduce duplication.

The main consideration is how serde handles it — you'd use `#[serde(flatten)]` to inline the fields during deserialization, which works well here since the metadata fields live at the same JSON level as the rest.

Something like:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct SessionMetadata {
    pub user_type: Option<String>,
    pub entrypoint: Option<String>,
    pub cwd: Option<String>,
    pub session_id: Option<String>,
    pub version: Option<String>,
    pub git_branch: Option<String>,
    pub slug: Option<String>,
}

// then in each record:
#[serde(flatten)]
pub metadata: Option<SessionMetadata>,
// or just:
#[serde(flatten)]
pub metadata: SessionMetadata,
```

Want to do that now or save it for later?

> DEVELOPER

I'm guessing the individual fields will always be present?

> AGENT

Right — the struct itself is always present (the fields are always in the JSON), it's the individual field *values* that might be absent. So:

```rust
#[serde(flatten)]
pub metadata: SessionMetadata,
```

With the `Option`s staying on the individual fields inside `SessionMetadata`.

> DEVELOPER

You think the individual fields would sometimes be absent?

> AGENT

Good push. Looking at the data, every record that has those metadata fields has *all* of them populated. They were probably `Option` out of excessive caution. Making them all required `String` is likely correct — and if a future file proves otherwise, the deserialize error will tell us exactly which field was missing.

Want to include that refactor (extract `SessionMetadata` with required fields) in this version, or save it for the next one?

> AGENT

There it is — a `text` block mixed in with `tool_result` blocks. Currently `UserContent::Blocks` expects `Vec<ToolResultBlock>`, but user content arrays can also contain `{"type": "text", "text": "..."}` entries.

The fix is to make the array items an enum that handles both `tool_result` and `text` block types. Single-step bump to **0.3.0**?

id: toolu_01YAXJv6d8cML3MBsczGxbv7
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs",
  "old_string": "/// User message content is either a plain text string (initial prompt)\n/// or an array of tool result blocks (tool responses).\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum UserContent {\n    Text(String),\n    Blocks(Vec<ToolResultBlock>),\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\npub struct ToolResultBlock {\n    pub tool_use_id: String,\n    #[serde(rename = \"type\")]\n    pub block_type: String,\n    pub content: ToolResultContent,\n    pub is_error: Option<bool>,\n}\n\n/// Tool result content can be a plain string or a structured value.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum ToolResultContent {\n    Text(String),\n    Structured(Value),\n}",
  "new_string": "/// User message content is either a plain text string (initial prompt)\n/// or an array of content blocks (tool results, text, etc.).\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum UserContent {\n    Text(String),\n    Blocks(Vec<UserContentBlock>),\n}\n\n/// A block within a user message content array.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\")]\npub enum UserContentBlock {\n    #[serde(rename = \"tool_result\")]\n    ToolResult {\n        tool_use_id: String,\n        content: ToolResultContent,\n        is_error: Option<bool>,\n    },\n\n    #[serde(rename = \"text\")]\n    Text { text: String },\n}\n\n/// Tool result content can be a plain string or a structured value.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum ToolResultContent {\n    Text(String),\n    Structured(Value),\n}"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

but I think we should have one or more test that asserts each variant was tested, thoughts

> AGENT

Good idea. We could add a test that deserializes all files and asserts every `Record` variant appeared at least once. That way if a trimmed file accidentally drops coverage of a variant, the test catches it.

Something like collecting a `HashSet<&str>` of variant labels and asserting it equals the full set of 9 types. One test across all files rather than per-file — keeps it simple.

Want me to add that?

> DEVELOPER

SG, also from the command line it would be nice to have -V for version and support wild cards, I assume not to hard?

> AGENT

Not hard at all. The shell already expands wildcards, so `ccs-viewer data/*.jsonl` will pass multiple file args — we just need to iterate over all args instead of taking only the first. For `-V`, a simple check before processing.

Should I keep it lightweight (manual arg handling) or pull in `clap`? Manual is simpler for now, but clap would be cleaner if we expect more flags later.

> DEVELOPER

Should we use clap?

> AGENT

I'd say yes — the app is growing and will likely gain more flags (output format, filtering, etc.). clap gives us `-V`/`--version` for free from `Cargo.toml`, proper `--help`, and clean multi-arg handling. The cost is one dependency but it's the standard choice for Rust CLIs.

> DEVELOPER

agree

> AGENT

This is a bigger change — version bump to **0.5.0**, single-step? Adding:

1. `clap` dependency with multiple file args + glob pattern support
2. `-V`/`--version` (free from clap)
3. Test asserting all 9 Record variants are covered

> AGENT

There they are — `umqqovro` and `zovkznpu` both titled "fix: make SystemRecord subtype-specific fields optional (0.4.0)". The first one (`zovkznpu`) was the initial commit, the second (`umqqovro`) was after adding the todo/trimming.

But `main` is already pushed and finalized. Since these are immutable, we'd need `--ignore-immutable` to squash them. Want me to fix it, or leave it as-is since it's just the `.claude` repo?

> DEVELOPER

fix it, no one else is using this. But why did it happen finalize is only on .claude and it should have squashed then pushed did you do 2 commits?

> AGENT

Yes, I did two commits to `.claude` — the first at the 0.4.0 commit (`zovkznpu`), then a second one (`umqqovro`) after we added the todo and trimmed the file. I should have squashed the second into the first instead of creating a new commit. Same mistake as the 0.2.0 round.

The lesson: when adding more changes to `.claude` in the same logical step, squash into the existing `.claude` commit rather than creating a new one.

     1→use std::collections::HashMap;
     2→use std::fs::File;
     3→use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
     4→
     5→use ccs_viewer::types::Record;
     6→
     7→fn main() {
     8→    let path = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| {
     9→        eprintln!("Usage: ccs-viewer <session.jsonl>");
    10→        std::process::exit(1);
    11→    });
    12→
    13→    let file = File::open(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    14→        eprintln!("Error opening {path}: {e}");
    15→        std::process::exit(1);
    16→    });
    17→
    18→    let reader = BufReader::new(file);
    19→    let mut counts: HashMap<&str, usize> = HashMap::new();
    20→    let mut errors: Vec<(usize, String)> = Vec::new();
    21→
    22→    for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
    23→        let line = line.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    24→            eprintln!("Error reading line {}: {e}", i + 1);
    25→            std::process::exit(1);
    26→        });
    27→        if line.trim().is_empty() {
    28→            continue;
    29→        }

    34→                    Record::User(_) => "user",
    35→                    Record::Assistant(_) => "assistant",
    36→                    Record::Progress(_) => "progress",
    37→                    Record::LastPrompt(_) => "last-prompt",
    38→                    Record::QueueOperation(_) => "queue-operation",
    39→                    Record::System(_) => "system",
    40→                    Record::CustomTitle(_) => "custom-title",
    41→                    Record::AgentName(_) => "agent-name",
    42→                };
    43→                *counts.entry(label).or_insert(0) += 1;
    44→            }
    45→            Err(e) => {

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs",
  "content": "use std::collections::HashMap;\nuse std::fs::File;\nuse std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse clap::Parser;\n\nuse ccs_viewer::types::Record;\n\n#[derive(Parser)]\n#[command(version, about = \"Claude Code session JSONL viewer\")]\nstruct Cli {\n    /// Session JSONL files to process\n    #[arg(required = true)]\n    files: Vec<PathBuf>,\n}\n\nfn record_label(record: &Record) -> &'static str {\n    match record {\n        Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => \"file-history-snapshot\",\n        Record::User(_) => \"user\",\n        Record::Assistant(_) => \"assistant\",\n        Record::Progress(_) => \"progress\",\n        Record::LastPrompt(_) => \"last-prompt\",\n        Record::QueueOperation(_) => \"queue-operation\",\n        Record::System(_) => \"system\",\n        Record::CustomTitle(_) => \"custom-title\",\n        Record::AgentName(_) => \"agent-name\",\n    }\n}\n\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n    let mut has_errors = false;\n\n    for path in &cli.files {\n        let file = File::open(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {\n            eprintln!(\"Error opening {}: {e}\", path.display());\n            std::process::exit(1);\n        });\n\n        let reader = BufReader::new(file);\n        let mut counts: HashMap<&str, usize> = HashMap::new();\n        let mut errors: Vec<(usize, String)> = Vec::new();\n\n        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {\n            let line = line.unwrap_or_else(|e| {\n                eprintln!(\"Error reading line {}: {e}\", i + 1);\n                std::process::exit(1);\n            });\n            if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                continue;\n            }\n            match serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line) {\n                Ok(record) => {\n                    *counts.entry(record_label(&record)).or_insert(0) += 1;\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                    errors.push((i + 1, format!(\"{e}\")));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        let total: usize = counts.values().sum();\n        println!(\"Deserialized {total} records from {}\", path.display());\n        for (label, count) in &counts {\n            println!(\"  {label}: {count}\");\n        }\n        if !errors.is_empty() {\n            has_errors = true;\n            eprintln!(\"\\n{} error(s):\", errors.len());\n            for (line_num, msg) in &errors {\n                eprintln!(\"  line {line_num}: {msg}\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if has_errors {\n        std::process::exit(1);\n    }\n}\n"
}

     1→use serde::Deserialize;

     4→/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file
     5→/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.
     6→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
     7→#[serde(tag = "type")]
     8→pub enum Record {
     9→    #[serde(rename = "file-history-snapshot")]
    10→    FileHistorySnapshot(FileHistorySnapshotRecord),
```

  "new_string": "impl Record {\n    /// Returns the type label string for this record variant.\n    pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => \"file-history-snapshot\",\n            Record::User(_) => \"user\",\n            Record::Assistant(_) => \"assistant\",\n            Record::Progress(_) => \"progress\",\n            Record::LastPrompt(_) => \"last-prompt\",\n            Record::QueueOperation(_) => \"queue-operation\",\n            Record::System(_) => \"system\",\n            Record::CustomTitle(_) => \"custom-title\",\n            Record::AgentName(_) => \"agent-name\",\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the full list of known record type labels.\n    pub fn all_labels() -> &'static [&'static str] {\n        &[\n            \"file-history-snapshot\",\n            \"user\",\n            \"assistant\",\n            \"progress\",\n            \"last-prompt\",\n            \"queue-operation\",\n            \"system\",\n            \"custom-title\",\n            \"agent-name\",\n        ]\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// file-history-snapshot\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------"
}

  "new_string": "    use super::*;\n    use std::collections::HashSet;\n    use std::fs::File;\n    use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};\n\n    const TEST_FILES: &[&str] = &[\n        \"data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl\",\n        \"data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl\",\n        \"data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl\",\n        \"data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl\",\n    ];\n\n    fn deserialize_file(path: &str) -> usize {\n        deserialize_file_collecting(path, None)\n    }\n\n    fn deserialize_file_collecting(path: &str, labels: Option<&mut HashSet<String>>) -> usize {\n        let file = File::open(path).expect(\"sample JSONL file should exist\");\n        let reader = BufReader::new(file);\n        let mut count = 0;\n        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {\n            let line = line.unwrap();\n            if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                continue;\n            }\n            let result = serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line);\n            assert!(\n                result.is_ok(),\n                \"line {} failed: {}\",\n                i + 1,\n                result.unwrap_err()\n            );\n            if let (Ok(record), Some(labels)) = (&result, labels.as_deref_mut()) {\n                labels.insert(record.label().to_string());\n            }\n            count += 1;\n        }\n        count\n    }"
}

}

> DEVELOPER

Another test, on a run keep track of the number of times each field is used, I want to verify that all fields have at least one occurance, thoughts?

> AGENT

Interesting idea. A few thoughts:

**What it would catch**: Fields that are *always* `None`/absent in our test data — meaning we've never actually verified serde can deserialize a real value into them. Essentially dead code in the structs.

**How to implement**: The simplest approach would be a test that deserializes all files and for each record, checks which `Option` fields are `Some` vs `None`, accumulating counts. Then assert every field has count > 0.

**The tricky part**: Getting field-level introspection without reflection. Rust doesn't have runtime reflection, so we'd need to either:
1. Write manual per-struct checking functions (tedious but explicit)
2. Deserialize to `serde_json::Value` in parallel and count which JSON keys appear (simpler, no struct coupling)
3. Use a derive macro (overkill)

I'd lean toward option 2 — deserialize each line as both `Record` (to verify it works) and `Value` (to collect field keys per record type). Then assert every field in our structs appears at least once in the `Value` data. No manual per-struct code needed, and it automatically adapts when we add fields.

Want to go with that approach?

> DEVELOPER

Option 2 wouldn't handle cases like timestamp which turns up many times and I'd want to catch cases where it wasn't "tested" in a particular structure

> AGENT

Good point. We need per-struct field tracking, not just global key counts. So `timestamp` in `SystemRecord` is tracked separately from `timestamp` in `QueueOperationRecord`.

Option 2 still works — we just key on `(record_type, field_name)`. Deserialize each line as `Value`, read the `"type"` field to get the record type, then collect all keys present. At the end, compare against expected fields per struct.

The expected fields could be a static map like:

```rust
("system", &["parentUuid", "isSidechain", "subtype", "timestamp", ...]),
("queue-operation", &["operation", "timestamp", "sessionId", ...]),
```

That does duplicate the struct definitions, but it's the test's job to verify them — and if someone adds a field to the struct but not the test, the test won't fail (safe direction). If someone adds a field to the test list but it never appears, the test catches it.

Want me to add this as part of 0.5.0 (squash in) or as a new version?

> DEVELOPER

The case of forgetting to add it to a test, feels like a false sense of secturity although not catastrophic as it is still being used. In the case of timestamp it's not optional if it's absent it'll fail at runtime?

> AGENT

Right. Required fields (`String`, `u64`, etc.) are already covered — if they're absent, serde fails and our existing tests catch that. The only fields worth tracking are `Option` ones, since those silently succeed as `None` whether or not we've ever seen a real value.

So the test narrows to: for each `Option` field in each struct, verify it's `Some` at least once across test data. That's the gap our current tests don't cover.

And for the "forgot to add to test" problem — we could flip it: instead of a static expected list, derive the `Option` field names from the `Value` approach. Look at all keys that *do* appear for a record type across all files, and compare against keys that appear in *every* record of that type. The difference tells us which fields are sometimes absent (i.e., `Option`). If an `Option` field is *never* present, that's the warning.

Though honestly the simpler version — a static list of `Option` fields per record type — is clear, easy to maintain, and the failure mode (forgetting to add a new field) is benign. Want to go with that?

> DEVELOPER

Ok the static list but add a warning in the code it doesn't catch fields that are always None if no test

> AGENT

   361→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);

   371→                "line {} failed: {}",
   372→                i + 1,
   373→                result.unwrap_err()
   374→            );
   375→            if let (Ok(record), Some(labels)) = (&result, labels.as_deref_mut()) {
   376→                labels.insert(record.label().to_string());
   377→            }
   378→            count += 1;
   379→        }
   380→        count
   381→    }

   364→    use super::*;
   365→    use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
   366→    use std::fs::File;

   368→

   370→        "data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl",
   371→        "data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl",

   376→    fn deserialize_file(path: &str) -> usize {

   379→
   380→    fn deserialize_file_collecting(path: &str, mut labels: Option<&mut HashSet<String>>) -> usize {
   381→        let file = File::open(path).expect("sample JSONL file should exist");
   382→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   383→        let mut count = 0;
   384→        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   385→            let line = line.unwrap();
   386→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   387→                continue;
   388→            }
   389→            let result = serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line);
   390→            assert!(
   391→                result.is_ok(),
   392→                "line {} failed: {}",
   393→                i + 1,
   394→                result.unwrap_err()
   395→            );
   396→            if let (Ok(record), Some(labels)) = (&result, labels.as_deref_mut()) {
   397→                labels.insert(record.label().to_string());
   398→            }
   399→            count += 1;
   400→        }
   401→        count
   402→    }
   403→
   404→    #[test]
   405→    fn deserialize_sample_31ba() {
   406→        assert_eq!(
   407→            deserialize_file("data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl"),
   408→            22
   409→        );
   410→    }
   411→
   412→    #[test]
   413→    fn deserialize_sample_997a() {
   414→        assert_eq!(
   415→            deserialize_file("data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl"),
   416→            461
   417→        );
   418→    }
   419→
   420→    #[test]
   421→    fn deserialize_sample_092d() {
   422→        assert_eq!(
   423→            deserialize_file("data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl"),
   424→            223
   425→        );
   426→    }
   427→
   428→    #[test]
   429→    fn deserialize_sample_86fb() {
   430→        assert_eq!(
   431→            deserialize_file("data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl"),
   432→            301
   433→        );
   434→    }
   435→
   436→    #[test]
   437→    fn all_variants_covered() {
   438→        let mut seen = HashSet::new();
   439→        for path in TEST_FILES {
   440→            deserialize_file_collecting(path, Some(&mut seen));
   441→        }
   442→        let expected: HashSet<String> =
   443→            Record::all_labels().iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect();
   444→        let missing: Vec<_> = expected.difference(&seen).collect();
   445→        assert!(
   446→            missing.is_empty(),
   447→            "Record variants not covered by test data: {missing:?}"
   448→        );
   449→    }
   450→}
   451→

Now add the test. The tricky part is handling the dot-path and `[type]` notation for nested/array fields. Let me add the test:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01WwfgFE1L5B4ErfXxoJHoja
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn all_variants_covered() {",
  "new_string": "    /// Look up a dotted path in a serde_json::Value.\n    /// Supports segments like \"message\", \"usage\", or array filtering\n    /// like \"content[tool_use]\" (find element in array where type == \"tool_use\").\n    fn value_at_path<'a>(val: &'a Value, path: &str) -> Option<&'a Value> {\n        let mut current = val;\n        for segment in path.split('.') {\n            if let Some(idx) = segment.find('[') {\n                let key = &segment[..idx];\n                let filter_type = &segment[idx + 1..segment.len() - 1];\n                let arr = current.get(key)?.as_array()?;\n                current = arr.iter().find(|el| {\n                    el.get(\"type\").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) == Some(filter_type)\n                })?;\n            } else {\n                current = current.get(segment)?;\n            }\n        }\n        if current.is_null() {\n            None\n        } else {\n            Some(current)\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Verify every Option field in every struct has at least one non-null\n    /// occurrence across test data. This catches Option fields that are defined\n    /// in a struct but never actually exercised by any test file.\n    ///\n    /// WARNING: This test uses a static list of Option fields per record type\n    /// (see each struct's `optional_fields()` method). It cannot catch Option\n    /// fields that are missing from those lists — those fields will silently\n    /// remain untested (always None). When adding a new Option field to a\n    /// struct, you must also add its camelCase JSON name to the corresponding\n    /// `optional_fields()` list.\n    #[test]\n    fn all_optional_fields_seen() {\n        let field_map = Record::optional_fields_by_type();\n        let mut seen: HashSet<(String, String)> = HashSet::new();\n\n        for path in TEST_FILES {\n            let file = File::open(path).expect(\"test file should exist\");\n            let reader = BufReader::new(file);\n            for line in reader.lines() {\n                let line = line.unwrap();\n                if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                    continue;\n                }\n                let val: Value = match serde_json::from_str(&line) {\n                    Ok(v) => v,\n                    Err(_) => continue,\n                };\n                let record_type = match val.get(\"type\").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {\n                    Some(t) => t,\n                    None => continue,\n                };\n                for (rtype, fields) in field_map {\n                    if *rtype != record_type {\n                        continue;\n                    }\n                    for field in *fields {\n                        if value_at_path(&val, field).is_some() {\n                            seen.insert((record_type.to_string(), field.to_string()));\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        let mut never_seen = Vec::new();\n        for (rtype, fields) in field_map {\n            for field in *fields {\n                if !seen.contains(&(rtype.to_string(), field.to_string())) {\n                    never_seen.push(format!(\"{rtype}.{field}\"));\n                }\n            }\n    557→    /// Verify every Option field in every struct has at least one non-null
   558→    /// occurrence across test data. This catches Option fields that are defined
   559→    /// in a struct but never actually exercised by any test file.
   560→    ///
   561→    /// WARNING: This test uses a static list of Option fields per record type
   562→    /// (see each struct's `optional_fields()` method). It cannot catch Option
   563→    /// fields that are missing from those lists — those fields will silently
   564→    /// remain untested (always None). When adding a new Option field to a
   565→    /// struct, you must also add its camelCase JSON name to the corresponding

`stop_reason` is in the JSON (snake_case, not camelCase) — because `AssistantMessage` doesn't use `rename_all = "camelCase"`. The field name in JSON matches the Rust field name. My path lookup uses camelCase but the JSON has snake_case for these API-level structs. Let me fix the paths:

> TOOL

> DEVELOPER

please explain how "message.xxx..." is used when you have a chance

> AGENT

The dot notation in `optional_fields()` describes the JSON path from the record root to the field. For example, an assistant record in JSON looks like:

```json
{
  "type": "assistant",
  "requestId": "...",
  "message": {
    "stop_reason": "end_turn",
    "usage": {
      "cache_creation": { ... }
    },
    "content": [
      { "type": "tool_use", "caller": { ... } }
    ]
  }
}
```

The test deserializes each line as a raw `serde_json::Value` (a JSON tree), then `value_at_path` walks the dots:

- `"message.stop_reason"` → `val["message"]["stop_reason"]`
- `"message.usage.cache_creation"` → `val["message"]["usage"]["cache_creation"]`
- `"message.content[tool_use].caller"` → find the element in `val["message"]["content"]` array where `type == "tool_use"`, then check `caller`

This is needed because `AssistantRecord` owns `AssistantMessage` which owns `Usage` — but in the JSON they're all nested. The test works on raw JSON, not deserialized Rust structs, so it needs the full path to find the field.

Now — `sourceToolAssistantUuid` genuinely never appears in our test data. That's a real gap the test caught. Our test data doesn't include any tool-result user records that reference the assistant that invoked the tool. We could either find a session file that has it, or accept it for now. What do you prefer?

> AGENT

There it is — line 588 has `"sourceToolAssistantUUID"` (note: all caps `UUID`, not `Uuid`). It's in the full `.claude/092de687` file which is *this current session*. And the key is `sourceToolAssistantUUID` not `sourceToolAssistantUuid` — that's a casing mismatch with `camelCase` rename. Let me check if our struct field would even match:

The struct has `source_tool_assistant_uuid` with `rename_all = "camelCase"`, which produces `sourceToolAssistantUuid`. But the JSON key is `sourceToolAssistantUUID`. That would mean serde *never* matches it — it silently falls through as `None`.

We need a `#[serde(rename = "sourceToolAssistantUUID")]` override. And the data file for testing needs to include those lines. Let me recopy the current session file (truncated to fit under 1 MiB) to replace the `data/092de687` file since it now has the field.

> AGENT

All 6 tests pass — including `all_optional_fields_seen` with `stop_sequence` now covered. serde ignores unknown fields by default so the extra fields are fine.

Every Option field is now tested. Ready to commit?

> DEVELOPER

So there weren't new fields?

> AGENT

There were new fields in the JSON (`container`, `isApiErrorMessage`, `context_management`), but serde's default behavior is `deny_unknown_fields` is **off** — it silently ignores JSON keys that don't map to a struct field. So they didn't cause errors, but they also aren't captured in our structs.

Whether we should add them depends on whether we'll need them. Want to add them now, or note them as a future TODO?

> DEVELOPER

we'll need "every" field possible, but we don't have to have that now but since we have a line with these new fields we "must" add them I vote turning on "deny_unknown_fields" so we absolutely know there is a problem with our code.

> AGENT

     4→/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file
     5→/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.

     7→#[serde(tag = "type")]

     9→    #[serde(rename = "file-history-snapshot")]

    11→

    13→    User(Box<UserRecord>),

    35→}
    36→
    37→impl Record {
    38→    /// Returns the type label string for this record variant.
    39→    pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {

    42→            Record::User(_) => "user",
    43→            Record::Assistant(_) => "assistant",
    44→            Record::Progress(_) => "progress",

    47→            Record::System(_) => "system",
    48→            Record::CustomTitle(_) => "custom-title",
    49→            Record::AgentName(_) => "agent-name",
    50→        }

```
     1→use std::collections::HashMap;
     2→use std::fs::File;
     3→use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
     4→use std::path::PathBuf;
     5→
     6→use clap::Parser;
     7→
     8→use ccs_viewer::types::Record;
     9→
    10→#[derive(Parser)]
    11→#[command(version, about = "Claude Code session JSONL viewer")]
    12→struct Cli {
    13→    /// Session JSONL files to process
    14→    #[arg(required = true)]
    15→    files: Vec<PathBuf>,
    16→}
    17→
    18→fn main() {
    19→    let cli = Cli::parse();
    20→    let mut has_errors = false;
    21→
    22→    for path in &cli.files {
    23→        let file = File::open(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    24→            eprintln!("Error opening {}: {e}", path.display());
    25→            std::process::exit(1);
    26→        });
    27→
    28→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);
    29→        let mut counts: HashMap<&str, usize> = HashMap::new();
    30→        let mut errors: Vec<(usize, String)> = Vec::new();
    31→
    32→        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
    33→            let line = line.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    34→                eprintln!("Error reading line {}: {e}", i + 1);
    35→                std::process::exit(1);
    36→            });
    37→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
    38→                continue;
    39→            }
    40→            match serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line) {
    41→                Ok(record) => {
    42→                    *counts.entry(record.label()).or_insert(0) += 1;
    43→                }
    44→                Err(e) => {
    45→                    errors.push((i + 1, format!("{e}")));
    46→                }
    47→            }
    48→        }
    49→
    50→        let total: usize = counts.values().sum();
    51→        println!("Deserialized {total} records from {}", path.display());
    52→        for (label, count) in &counts {
    53→            println!("  {label}: {count}");
    54→        }
    55→        if !errors.is_empty() {
    56→            has_errors = true;
    57→            eprintln!("\n{} error(s):", errors.len());
    58→            for (line_num, msg) in &errors {
    59→                eprintln!("  line {line_num}: {msg}");
    60→            }
    61→        }
    62→    }
    63→
    64→    if has_errors {
    65→        std::process::exit(1);
    66→    }
    67→}
    68→
```

looking at impl Record::label and Record::all_labels the have the same set of strings, do we need both?

> AGENT

Good catch. `all_labels()` could just be derived from calling `label()` on each variant. But since we don't have instances of each variant lying around, the simplest approach is to keep `all_labels()` and have `label()` index into it. Or — we could drop `all_labels()` and have the `all_variants_covered` test build the expected set differently.

Actually, the cleanest fix: keep `all_labels()` as the single source of truth, and make `label()` return from that same list. But matching an enum variant to an index is awkward.

Simplest: drop `all_labels()`, and in the test just hardcode the expected count (9 variants) instead of comparing against a list. Or have the test use `Record::optional_fields_by_type()` keys plus the types that have no optional fields.

What do you think — drop `all_labels()` and just assert `seen.len() == 9` in the test?

> DEVELOPER

I if both are deseriable I'd have label use all_labels so as we DRY (Don't Repeat Yourself), thoughts

> AGENT

Makes sense. `label()` can index into `all_labels()` by matching the variant to a position:

```rust
pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
    Record::all_labels()[match self {
        Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => 0,
        Record::User(_) => 1,
        // ...
    }]
}
```

But that's fragile — the index must stay in sync with the array order. An alternative: match on the variant and return the same `&'static str` from `all_labels()` by searching:

Actually, the simplest DRY approach is the opposite direction — drop `all_labels()` and derive it. We can't iterate enum variants natively, but we can keep a const array and have `label()` return from it:

```rust
const LABELS: &[&str] = &[
    "file-history-snapshot", "user", "assistant", ...
];

pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
    LABELS[match self {
        Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => 0,
        Record::User(_) => 1,
        ...
    }]
}

pub fn all_labels() -> &'static [&'static str] {
    LABELS
}
```

One source of truth for the strings. The index approach is a bit brittle but the compiler ensures the match is exhaustive, so adding a variant without updating the match is a compile error. Want me to do this?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs",
  "old_string": "use serde::Deserialize;\nuse serde_json::Value;\n\n/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file\n/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.",
  "new_string": "//! Serde types for Claude Code session JSONL files.\n//!\n//! Each line of a session file is a JSON object with a `\"type\"` field that\n//! determines its shape. The [`Record`] enum uses serde's internally tagged\n//! representation (`#[serde(tag = \"type\")]`) to dispatch to the correct\n//! variant struct.\n//!\n//! # Key patterns\n//!\n//! **Label strings** — The `RECORD_LABELS` const is the single source of\n//! truth for type name strings (e.g. `\"assistant\"`, `\"system\"`).\n//! [`Record::label()`] indexes into it; [`Record::all_labels()`] returns\n//! the full slice. This avoids duplicating strings between the two methods.\n//!\n//! **Struct field ordering** — Each struct lists required fields first,\n//! then `Option` fields below a separator comment. This makes it visually\n//! obvious which fields need entries in the `optional_fields()` list.\n//!\n//! **`deny_unknown_fields`** — Every struct has `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`\n//! so that any JSON key not mapped to a Rust field causes a deserialization\n//! error. This ensures we know immediately when Claude Code adds new fields.\n//!\n//! **`optional_fields()`** — Structs with `Option` fields have a companion\n//! `optional_fields()` method listing the camelCase (or snake_case) JSON\n//! names of those fields. The `all_optional_fields_seen` test uses these\n//! lists to verify every `Option` field is `Some` at least once in test\n//! data. Nested fields use dot notation (`\"message.usage.speed\"`) and\n//! array filtering uses brackets (`\"message.content[tool_use].caller\"`).\n//!\n//! **Warning**: The `optional_fields()` lists are static — adding an\n//! `Option` field to a struct without updating the list means that field\n//! silently goes untested. The separator comment in each struct serves as\n//! a reminder.\n\nuse serde::Deserialize;\nuse serde_json::Value;\n\n/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file\n/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field."
}

```
     1→# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
     2→
     3→## Memory vs CLAUDE.md
     4→
     5→Prefer CLAUDE.md for project instructions and conventions — it is
     6→checked into the repo and portable. Use memory/ only for user-specific
     7→context that does not belong in the codebase.
     8→
     9→## Project Structure
    10→
    11→This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
    12→
    13→1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
    14→2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
    15→
    16→Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
    17→
    18→## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
    19→
    20→- App repo: `.` (project root)
    21→- Bot session repo: `.claude`
    22→  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
    23→
    24→## Working Directory
    25→
    26→Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
    27→to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
    28→discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
    29→command failures downstream.
    30→
    31→## Committing
    32→
    33→Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
    34→relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
    35→visible at the start of the command.
    36→
    37→### App repo
    38→```
    39→jj commit -m \
    40→"title" \
    41→-m "body
    42→
    43→ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
    44→-R .
    45→```
    46→
    47→### Bot session repo
    48→```
    49→jj commit -m \
    50→"title" \
    51→-m "body
    52→
    53→ochid: /<changeID>" \
    54→-R .claude
    55→```
    56→
    57→## jj Basics
    58→
    59→- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
    60→- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
    61→- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
    62→- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
    63→- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
    64→  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
    65→- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
    66→  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
    67→- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
    68→  session because session data updates continuously.
    69→
    70→## Commit Message Style
    71→
    72→Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
    73→a version suffix:
    74→
    75→```
    76→<type>: <short description> (<version>)
    77→```
    78→
    79→- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
    80→  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
    81→  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
    82→- **Body**: expand on *what* if needed, plus short *why* and *how*.
    83→- Examples:
    84→  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
    85→  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
    86→  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
    87→
    88→## Pre-commit Requirements
    89→
    90→### User approval
    91→
    92→Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
    93→user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
    94→them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
    95→pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
    96→
    97→### Notes references
    98→
    99→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
   100→See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
   101→
   102→### Versioning
   103→
   104→Every change must start with a version bump. See
   105→[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
   106→for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
   107→
   108→### Pre-commit checklist
   109→
   110→Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
   111→
   112→1. `cargo fmt`
   113→2. `cargo clippy`
   114→3. `cargo test`
   115→4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
   116→5. Retest after install
   117→6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
   118→7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
   119→8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
   120→   new subcommands, changed behavior)
   121→
   122→## ochid Trailers
   123→
   124→Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
   125→counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
   126→path followed by the **12-character** changeID (the full short form from
   127→`vc-x1 chid -L`, not the truncated prefix jj shows in logs):
   128→
   129→- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
   130→- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
   131→
   132→Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both 12-character changeIDs
   133→(first line is app repo, second is `.claude`).
   134→
   135→## Session End Workflows
   136→
   137→When the user asks to "commit both repos" or says they're done, commit
   138→both repos. Use the **same title** for both commits so they're easy to
   139→correlate. The body can differ: the app repo body should summarize code
   140→changes; the bot session repo body should note what was done in the
   141→session.
   142→
   143→```
   144→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
   145→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
   146→```
   147→
   148→After both commits, **pause and ask for user approval** before
   149→proceeding to bookmark/push/finalize.
   150→
   151→When the user also asks to push, advance the current bookmark on both
   152→repos, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude` here —
   153→`finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
   154→
   155→Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
   156→`dev-0.14.0`).
   157→
   158→```
   159→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   160→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
   161→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   162→```
   163→
   164→### Late changes after push
   165→
   166→If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
   167→updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
   168→`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
   169→
   170→```
   171→jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
   172→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   173→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   174→```
   175→
   176→### Finalize the .claude repo
   177→
   178→The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
   179→This squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The
   180→delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
   181→short relative path for `--repo`.
   182→
   183→**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
   184→calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
   185→finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
   186→
   187→`--bookmark` is required — use the active bookmark for the session.
   188→
   189→```
   190→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --push
   191→```
   192→
   193→Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
   194→already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
   195→miss the finalize squash window.
   196→
```
